Connections across the record

Follow each relationship, its date and its evidence. A path can cross different periods; it does not by itself establish shared intent or continuing control.

Curated connection paths

The Taj creditor network, 1988–1991

Rothschild Inc. advised Taj bondholders in the restructuring. The bondholders negotiated with the casino business to which Trump had contributed capital.

  1. Rothschild Inc. → Taj bondholders

    Creditor adviser · 1990–91 · Evidence [B]

    Read the supporting record

    • TIME: Wall Street: The Dealers Return1991-09-16 · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: Opening article passage
    • United Press International: Trump's Taj Mahal casino to enter consensual bankruptcy1990-11-16 · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: Indexed article text naming Ross as a key negotiator and describing announced terms
    • The Washington Post: Speculators Are Enjoying the Spectacle of Trump's Taj Mahal Troubles1990-11-12 · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: Displayed date Nov. 12, 1990; HTML lines 19-28
  2. Taj bondholders → Taj Associates

    Creditor negotiations · 1990 · Evidence [B]

    Read the supporting record

    • TIME: Wall Street: The Dealers Return1991-09-16 · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: Opening article passage
    • United Press International: Trump's Taj Mahal casino to enter consensual bankruptcy1990-11-16 · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: Indexed article text naming Ross as a key negotiator and describing announced terms
    • The Washington Post: Speculators Are Enjoying the Spectacle of Trump's Taj Mahal Troubles1990-11-12 · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: Displayed date Nov. 12, 1990; HTML lines 19-28
  3. THE PRINCIPAL → Taj Associates

    $75m capital contribution · 1988 · Evidence [A]

    Read the supporting record

    • U.S. District Court for the District of New Jersey; Justia mirror: In re Donald J. Trump Casino Securities Litigation, 793 F. Supp. 543Publication date not recorded · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: 793 F. Supp. 543; MDL 864 (JFG); Civ. A. No. 90-0919; prospectus dated Nov. 9, 1988; HTML lines 73-78, 195-211, 258-271, 361-383

The capital contribution and creditor negotiations are separately dated relationships. Creditor-side advisory work does not establish a personal loan from a Rothschild family member or continuing institutional control.

Next check: Retrieve the creditor engagement, committee appointment and fee orders to establish the precise legal mandate and its duration.

Wilbur Ross: creditor adviser to Commerce secretary

The same person connects the earlier creditor negotiations with a later Trump administration appointment.

  1. Wilbur Ross → Rothschild Inc.

    Executive managing director · 1990 · Evidence [A]

    Read the supporting record

    • Mittal Steel Company N.V., filed through U.S. SEC EDGAR: Mittal Steel Company N.V. Shareholders Circular dated March 14, 20052005-03-14 · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: Exhibit 99.1.1 to Form 6-K, accession 0000950123-05-003084; Wilbur L. Ross biographical paragraph
  2. THE PRINCIPAL → Wilbur Ross

    Administration: Commerce secretary · 2017 · Evidence [A]

    Read the supporting record

    • United States Senate: U.S. Senate Roll Call Vote 73, confirmation of Wilbur L. Ross, Jr. to be Secretary of Commerce2017-02-27 · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: Nomination PN32; vote 73; confirmed 72-27 on 2017-02-27
    • Trump White House, archived by the National Archives: Wilbur L. Ross, Jr. — Secretary of CommercePublication date not recorded · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: Biography opening; sworn-in date, office, Trump administration role, and prior WL Ross & Co. role

The SEC-filed biography dates the end of Ross’s Rothschild Inc. employment to March 2000; his Commerce appointment followed in February 2017. The path is a personnel connection across time. A continuing institutional mandate or influence over the appointment requires separate evidence.

Next check: Check dated appointment ethics records and disclosed interests for a specific continuing relationship; former employment alone does not establish one.

    Chairman appointment · Aug 2025 filing · Evidence [A]

Corporate donations and a presidential reception offer

Chevron’s contribution record and Freedom 250’s sponsorship brochure connect a named payer, a fundraising entity and an offered presidential reception.

  1. Chevron U.S.A. Inc. → Freedom 250 LLC

    $5M · May 28, 2026 · Evidence [A]

    Read the supporting record

    • Clerk of the House and Secretary of the Senate: Chevron U.S.A. Inc. midyear 2026 LD-203 contribution report2026-07-30 · reviewed 2026-09-23Location: Honorary Expenses row #109, May 28, 2026
  2. Freedom 250 LLC → THE PRINCIPAL

    Brochure offered Trump-hosted reception · $1M+ · Evidence [A]

    Read the supporting record

    • Freedom 250, mirrored by The New York Times: Freedom 250 sponsorship brochurePublication date not recorded · reviewed 2026-09-23Location: Undated brochure; PDF pp. 6, 8, 10, 12-13; offered reception and photo opportunity
    • U.S. Senators Warren, Schiff, Padilla and Blumenthal: Senators request Freedom 250 donor and spending records2026-09-14 · reviewed 2026-09-23Location: Eight-page letter; pp. 1, 3-4, 6-8; released September 14
    • Delaware Division of Corporations, mirrored by The New York Times: Certificate of formation of Freedom 250 LLCPublication date not recorded · reviewed 2026-09-23Location: One-page certificate; filed October 28, 2025; file number 10374042

These records establish payments and a marketed benefit. They leave the consequential question open: who actually attended, what was discussed, and whether any official decision was exchanged for a contribution. The path supports investigation of that sequence without asserting the exchange occurred.

Next check: Match donor records to actual event invitations, attendee lists, communications and dated federal decisions; assess any organization response to the September 28 request.

All reviewed relationships

Björn Höcke → AfD Thüringen

State spokesperson · 2024 court record · Evidence [A]

  • VG Weimar rejected AfD Thüringen’s challenge to three passages in the 2021 state intelligence report. The court’s 28 August 2024 release says the agency could characterize a remembrance post by state spokesperson Höcke, which omitted Holocaust victims, as historical revisionism.

    This is the court’s summary of oral reasons in 8 K 1272/23 We, an administrative reporting dispute. The release expressly said the judgment was not final and written reasons were not yet available. A later appellate disposition remains unresolved. The source identifies AfD Thüringen as the regional party organization and Höcke as its state spokesperson; it does not establish a criminal Holocaust-denial conviction.

  • Verwaltungsgericht Weimar / Thüringer Verwaltungsgerichtsbarkeit: Klage des Landesverbandes Thüringen der AfD gegen drei Textpassagen in dem Verfassungsschutzbericht 2021 erfolglos2024-08-28 · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: judgment and release of 2024-08-28, 8 K 1272/23 We; oral reasons summarized; release expressly says not final

Rainer Lanzerath → AfD

Member · conduct in 2018 · Evidence [A]

  • The European Court of Human Rights records that AfD member Rainer Edmund Maria Lanzerath was convicted in Augsburg on 23 August 2019 and fined €4,500 for two counts of Holocaust minimization under § 130(3) StGB.

    His banner and earlier online image compared a yellow Star of David marked 1933–1945 with the AfD logo marked 2013–?. He argued that he was protesting marginalization of AfD members. The courts found the comparison blatantly minimized the Holocaust. The decision identifies individual membership and a congress setting; it does not establish party commissioning or adoption, or Holocaust denial.

  • European Court of Human Rights: Lanzerath v. Germany, application no. 1854/222022-07-28 · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: decision 2022-07-05; paragraphs 1-15; notified 2022-07-28

Read report context →

Jörg Meuthen → AfD

Federal spokesperson · 2020–21 · Evidence [A]

  • AfD’s 26 January 2020 remembrance statement identifies Jörg Meuthen and Tino Chrupalla as its federal spokespeople and attributes to them an acknowledgment of the Nazi murder of European Jews.

    This dated party record establishes the named speakers and their published position. It is not evidence that every member shared that position or that these were their offices at all later dates.

  • In a 9 March 2021 party statement, Meuthen and Chrupalla condemned Holocaust denial and minimization and said the federal board had initiated exclusion proceedings against a Bavarian member over a vaccine–Zyklon B comparison.

    This is the party’s account of its stated position and steps it said it took. The statement does not establish completed expulsion, every member’s beliefs, or the party’s entire present position. It is relevant counterevidence to a blanket claim that the national party denies the Holocaust.

  • Alternative für Deutschland: Meuthen and Chrupalla: remembrance of victims of National Socialism2020-01-26 · reviewed 2026-09-08

THE PRINCIPAL → Taj Associates

$75m capital contribution · 1988 · Evidence [A]

  • The 1992 federal securities opinion records a $75 million capital contribution by Donald Trump to the Taj partnership and identifies Merrill Lynch as the 1988 bond underwriter.

    The reproduced prospectus table lists $675 million in bond proceeds, $75 million in capital and $55 million from other sources. The opinion’s opening background instead says $674 million; that source discrepancy is retained. The court says Trump made the required contribution and was not a personal guarantor of the Taj’s success. This does not resolve every separate debt or guarantee.

Taj Funding Inc. → Taj Associates

Affiliated co-debtors · July 1991 · Evidence [A]

  • A 1993 bankruptcy opinion records that Trump Taj Mahal Associates and three affiliated corporate debtors filed Chapter 11 petitions on 16 July 1991; the court confirmed their plan on 28 August 1991.

    The named affiliates were Trump Taj Mahal, Inc., The Trump Taj Mahal Corporation and Trump Taj Mahal Funding, Inc. These are entity bankruptcies. This opinion supplies the filing and confirmation dates; it does not establish every creditor’s role, final distribution or a personal Trump bankruptcy.

THE PRINCIPAL → Ladder Finance LLC

Disclosed TIHT mortgage · 2025 · Evidence [A]

  • Trump’s certified annual disclosure for 2025 lists Ladder Capital Finance LLC’s TIHT mortgage at $5,000,001–$25,000,000 with a 2026 maturity, two Axos mortgages above $50 million each with 2032 maturities, and a Charles Schwab Bank pledged-asset line above $50 million incurred in 2025.

    Part 8 also reports the Bryn Mawr Seven Springs and Professional Bank South Ocean mortgages paid off in July 2025, and Ladder Capital Finance I LLC’s 40 Wall Street mortgage paid off in June 2025. The two Ladder names are distinct. These are the filer’s reported liabilities, not live lender balances or proof of the exact legal obligor on each underlying instrument. OGE released the certified report on 30 June 2026; it does not establish status on 8 September 2026.

TREASURY · BESSENT → OFAC

Treasury office issuing license · Sep 2026 · Evidence [A]

  • Treasury’s OFAC issued General License CC on September 4, 2026. It permits specified wind-down transactions involving the three named Golden Global entities and entities they own at least 50 percent of, individually or together, through 12:01 a.m. EDT on September 19, 2026.

    Payments to blocked persons must enter a blocked, interest-bearing U.S. account. Paragraph (b) retains other prohibitions under E.O. 13902 unless separately authorized. The license is a limited exception to sanctions, not an unblocking order or a general permission to continue business. The expiry is a scheduled boundary, not an observed future event.

  • U.S. Treasury, Office of Foreign Assets Control: Iran General License CC2026-09-04 · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: Complete one-page license; paragraphs (a)(1)–(4), payment condition and (b); director signature and date

Documented record · Official license and scheduled expiry

Treasury’s OFAC issued General License CC on September 4, 2026. It permits specified wind-down transactions involving the three named Golden Global entities and entities they own at least 50 percent of, individually or together, through 12:01 a.m. EDT on September 19, 2026.

Payments to blocked persons must enter a blocked, interest-bearing U.S. account. Paragraph (b) retains other prohibitions under E.O. 13902 unless separately authorized. The license is a limited exception to sanctions, not an unblocking order or a general permission to continue business. The expiry is a scheduled boundary, not an observed future event.

VG Weimar rejected AfD Thüringen’s challenge to three passages in the 2021 state intelligence report. The court’s 28 August 2024 release says the agency could characterize a remembrance post by state spokesperson Höcke, which omitted Holocaust victims, as historical revisionism.

Documented record · Court finding

LG Halle found that Höcke deliberately prompted an audience in Gera to complete the prohibited SA slogan on 12 December 2023. Its 1 July 2024 judgment imposed 130 daily units of €130.

According to the court, he spoke the opening words and gestured for the audience’s completion while the earlier prosecution was pending. The completion was attributable to him. This final prohibited-symbol conviction must be distinguished from Holocaust denial or minimization.

Documented record · Court finding

The European Court of Human Rights unanimously declared Lanzerath’s application 1854/22 inadmissible on 5 July 2022, with written notification on 28 July.

The four-page decision records the unsuccessful domestic appeals and the Federal Constitutional Court’s 21 September 2021 refusal to accept the constitutional complaint without reasons. It treats the conviction as a proportionate interference with expression. It does not attribute the member’s banner to the federal party.

Documented record · Court finding

LG Halle found that Björn Höcke knowingly used the prohibited SA slogan at the end of a campaign speech in Merseburg on 29 May 2021. Its 14 May 2024 judgment imposed 100 daily units of €130.

The court found deliberate public use and an effort to return the slogan to ordinary political language. The offense was use of prohibited symbols under § 86a StGB. The court’s September 2025 release reports the conviction as final; this is not a Holocaust-denial finding.

Documented record · Court finding

The European Court of Human Rights records that AfD member Rainer Edmund Maria Lanzerath was convicted in Augsburg on 23 August 2019 and fined €4,500 for two counts of Holocaust minimization under § 130(3) StGB.

His banner and earlier online image compared a yellow Star of David marked 1933–1945 with the AfD logo marked 2013–?. He argued that he was protesting marginalization of AfD members. The courts found the comparison blatantly minimized the Holocaust. The decision identifies individual membership and a congress setting; it does not establish party commissioning or adoption, or Holocaust denial.

These are the written institutional rules in Articles I and III. Their existence alone does not demonstrate present compliance; that requires dated statutes, orders, judgments and implementation records.

Historical comparisons

A comparison records its evidence and limits. It is not an outcome forecast.

Institutional sequences across historical cases

Historical case
Germany, 1933: appointment and subsequent institutional changes
Documented basis
The historical reference distinguishes appointment to office from direct election to that office.
Similarities
No contemporary equivalence has been established in this comparison record.
Material differences
Jurisdiction, constitutional rules, institutions, time period and observed outcomes must be specified for each proposed comparison.
Evidence against the comparison
Cases in which similar pressures were constrained, reversed or produced different outcomes must be examined alongside the proposed case.
Next record to check
Add original historical and contemporary records before asserting a shared mechanism.
  • United States Holocaust Memorial Museum: Hitler Comes to PowerPublication date not recorded · reviewed 2026-09-08

Emergency authority: test the mechanism, then its limits

Historical case
Germany, March 1933: a law authorized government legislation outside ordinary parliamentary procedure.
Documented basis
The Enabling Act’s Articles 1–3 specify the transfer and its stated exceptions.
Similarities
A useful research question is whether a present measure bypasses ordinary lawmaking, and how institutions respond. A shared mechanism has to be proved in the specific records.
Material differences
The U.S. Constitution allocates powers separately. Rhetoric, an executive order and the 1933 legislative transfer are different acts; the authority and legal effect of each must be examined.
Evidence against the comparison
An effective judicial limit, legislative refusal, reversal or verified compliance would weaken a claim that constraints have ceased to function. A ruling alone does not establish compliance.
Next record to check
For each current controversy, join the exact instrument to the operative ruling and later compliance record. Record what would strengthen and what would disprove the proposed comparison before checking the next event.

  • National Archives: The Constitution of the United States: A TranscriptionPublication date not recorded · reviewed 2026-09-08Location: Article I, sections 1 and 7; Article II, sections 2–3; Article III, sections 1–2.
